# Agent scorecard — how good were the audit agents, really?

Floris 2026-06-25: while hand-certifying every page (see `CERT_LOG.md`), score the agents. For every page: did the agents **miss** a real error (called it clean but it wasn't)? When they **fixed** something, was it a real error fixed correctly, a **false positive** (fixed a non-error / reverted a correct reading), or a **botched fix** (real error, wrong correction)? Attribute every verdict to the workflow + prompt that produced it.

## How the certification auto-scores the agents
- A page's current `.tex` differs from the `repair032` baseline exactly where a fix (agent or manual) was applied (see `_work/swarm_results/workpass_vs_repair032.diff`).
- If my hand-pass **certifies the page clean** (current `.tex` == scan):
  - page WAS changed → the applied fix(es) were **CORRECT** ✓
  - page was NOT changed → baseline was already right; if an agent called it clean, agent **CORRECT** ✓
- If my hand-pass **finds an error**:
  - on a changed line → **BOTCHED FIX** ✗ (or incomplete)
  - on an unchanged line → **MISS** ✗ (agent/baseline both wrong)

I zoom-confirmed the scan: `c'` in the application, `d'` in the citation — the source literally contradicts itself across two adjacent lines. **Why this matters for grading the agents:** every prior `.tex` deviation I've found was either the agents *over*-faithfully copying a source quirk (good) or making a defensible prose typo-fix (good); this is the first case where being faithful to the source *propagated a source error* into the edition. The swarm cannot be faulted for it — its job was `.tex`-vs-scan fidelity, and the `.tex` matches the scan exactly — but it does show that a pure fidelity pass (agent or human) will silently carry the source's own mistakes, which is precisely why a separate *mathematical* read (does `d'` even exist here?) is necessary on top of transcription-checking. I corrected `d'→c'` in the working file (math-forced, unambiguous, self-contradicted by the source) rather than only flagging it, because the standing goal is a base a mathematician can *trust*, an undefined symbol in a formula breaks that, and the working `.tex` is exactly where corrections belong as reviewable diffs. This is distinct from the "2.6.2" / capital-`P` quirks, which I *kept*: those are harmless notational/reference idiosyncrasies the reader parses without confusion, whereas `g'_*d'^!` is a wrong symbol in an equation. - **p146 — IIIB's first diagrams, and the most trust-relevant defect class yet: dropped diagram labels.** §2 opens with two commutative square-diagrams (D071 the a-version, D072 the b-version) plus Lemme 2.2 and the filtration setup. The diagrams' **objects and arrows are all correct** — but the source labels their four squares `(1),(2),(3),(4)` and then *refers to them by number* in the surrounding prose (`les carrés (1) et (4) sont cartésiens`; later `comme a_2 est fini et le carré (1) cartésien`), and the `.tex` tikzcd had dropped those labels. This is the 6th `.tex` fix and a new defect class — and the sharpest one for the project's thesis after p143: a dropped graphical label is invisible to a compile check (compiles fine), invisible to a `.tex`-vs-scan *text* diff (it's not text in the body), and invisible to an agent that doesn't look at the rendered diagram against the source. Only eyes on both diagrams catch it. Combined with p143's copied-typo, p146 cements that IIIB needed exactly this kind of pass. Also documented an 8th source typo (`M|U` for `M|V` in Lemme 2.2 b), `.tex` already correct). IIIB defect taxonomy now: (a) `.tex`-introduced rendering slips [p138, p141]; (b) copied source typo [p143]; (c) cosmetic style [p144]; (d) **dropped diagram labels [p146]** — plus source typos the `.tex` caught [p146]. - **p147 — three source typos on one page, the `.tex` read through all three: the two-axis read of IIIB crystallizes.** hand-CLEAN ✓; no diagram, no fix. This page of Illusie's typescript is unusually error-prone — within a few lines it mislabels a map arrow (`u` for `v`), writes `gr^1 M` for `gr^0 M`, and writes a projection `s→S` for `x→S` (plus a spelling slip `au`/`aux`) — and the transcription silently corrected **every one** to the intended object, all confirmed at 2.55× zoom. That matters for the scorecard because it cleanly separates two skills the IIIB transcriber clearly had to different degrees: (1) *mathematical comprehension* — reading a typo'd source and writing what it means — where IIIB is **excellent**, matching Exposé III's "reads through the slips" record (now 9 source typos the `.tex` caught, vs 2 it copied); and (2) *presentational fidelity* — preserving headers, diagram labels, exact symbols — where IIIB is **looser** (the p138/p141/p146 defects). So the right characterization of IIIB is not "noisier transcription" flatly, but specifically: the mathematics was understood and is trustworthy, while the typesetting carried more drift. A reader can trust the *content* of the IIIB `.tex` as much as Exp III's; what needed fixing was the *rendering*. That is a genuinely reassuring finding for a base-of-trust deliverable — the errors were never in the math. Source-typo tally now 11 (9 `.tex`-correct, 2 copied+fixed). Ledger: 147 pages, 6 `.tex` fixes, 11 source typos found (9 `.tex`-correct, 2 copied+fixed), 1 cross-ref quirk + 1 stylistic note, 0 content transcription errors, 72 diagrams perfect.
- **p148 — dropped referenced labels confirmed as a *systematic* IIIB defect.** §2.3's proof finishes and Lemme 2.4 opens — all content exact — but the three local display equations the source numbers `(1)`, `(2)`, `(3)`, and which the prose then cites (`d'après (1)`, `la conclusion découle de la conjugaison de (2) et (3)`), came through the `.tex` as untagged displays. So, just as on p146 (where the source's square labels (1)–(4) were dropped from the diagrams the prose referenced), the `.tex` here asserts "by the conjugation of (2) and (3)" with no (2) or (3) on the page. Two instances two pages apart, both of the *same shape* — the transcriber strips local cross-reference labels (`\tag`s, diagram square numbers) that the surrounding argument depends on — promotes this from "isolated slip" to a **named, systematic IIIB rendering defect**. Recorded a standing flag to do a global label-audit of IIIB at packaging (grep referenced `(n)`/`carré (n)` against present tags). Restored the three `\tag`s; gate held. This is the most consequential IIIB defect class for trust, because unlike a stray header or a capital-Z it makes the *argument itself* unfollowable in the `.tex` — and like the p146/p143 finds, it is invisible to compile and to text-diff, surfacing only when a human reads the prose's "(2)" and looks for it in the displays. 7th `.tex` fix. Content remains 0-error. (2) Just as important for the scorecard: **the dropped-label defect did NOT occur here** — the page's one numbered display, (2.4.1), carries its tag correctly, and the other displays are unnumbered in the source too. So the p146/p148 label-dropping is **intermittent, not universal**: the transcriber dropped labels on some diagram/equation displays but not others. That's actually the more demanding finding operationally — if it were every page, a blanket fix would do; because it's page-by-page unpredictable, the only safe remedy is the standing global label-audit (grep every referenced `(n)`/`carré (n)` against present tags) plus the per-page eyes-on check the grind already does. The copied-typo count (now 3) vs `.tex`-caught count (9) still says the transcription understood the math far more often than it slipped; the slips it did make are being caught one page at a time. The two notations denote the same object, so this is meaning-preserving — but the internal inconsistency is the tell that it's a transcription slip, not a deliberate normalization, so it gets fixed (like p141's `Z`). Worth noting the **diagnostic heuristic** this confirms: when the `.tex` writes a symbol one way in a display and the parallel/adjacent display the other way, the odd one out is almost always a `.tex` deviation — a cheap signal to zoom. The IIIB `.tex`-deviation defects now sort cleanly into two families: **(a) dropped referenced labels** (p146 diagram squares, p148 equation numbers — intermittent, needs the global audit) and **(b) notation deviations** (p141 capital-Z, p150 spurious-`b^*` — usually flagged by an internal inconsistency). Both are presentation-layer; neither has touched the mathematics, which remains 0-error across 150 pages. The grind is finding ~1 such `.tex` slip every ~1.5 pages in IIIB §2 specifically — denser than §1, consistent with §2 being the most diagram-and-display-heavy stretch so far. The fix: `f:T-Z↪T` should be `f:T-Y↪T` — a `Z`-for-`Y` slip in the source the `.tex` copied, forced by the parallel `f':Z-Y↪Z` and the chain `T-(X∪Y)↪T-Y↪T`. With p143 (`(DM)_x` for `(DL)_x`), p149 (`M'` for `M`), and now p151 (`T-Z` for `T-Y`), the **four copied-source-typos share one signature**: a single wrong letter/symbol that breaks either a *type* (an object with no fiber/meaning in context) or a *parallel* (an index that doesn't match its mirror). That's a nameable, reproducible detection method — not "read carefully" but "re-derive the type and check the mirror" — and it's exactly what a text-diff against the scan cannot do (the `.tex` *agrees* with the scan; both are wrong). It's the strongest recurring justification for the by-hand pass. Running split: 13 source typos, `.tex` caught 9 (69%), copied 4 (31%); every copied one was a structure-breaking single-symbol slip. **§2 sub-verdict:** six pages (p146–151) carrying the reduction to the vanishing statement (2.6.4) — content faithful (0 transcription errors), but the densest `.tex`-defect stretch yet (6 fixes across 6 pages: 2 dropped-label, 2 notation-deviation, 2 copied-typo), all presentation/structure, none mathematical. The math a reader would rely on is intact; the scaffolding (labels, parallel symbols) is where the transcription frayed and the by-hand pass repaired. The divisibility theorem (Grothendieck XII 4.5) the whole exposé was built to prove.** hand-CLEAN ✓, no fix, no source typo. §6.10 states `(6.10.2) Tr_Λ(u)=|G|·Tr_{Λ[G]}(u)(e)`: the Lefschetz–Verdier local term of an equivariant correspondence is divisible by `|G|`, with the value-at-`e` of the finer `Λ[G]`-valued local term as quotient — plus the `|Z_g|` centralizer refinement `(6.10.3)–(6.10.5)`. Two things worth recording. (1) **The verification was *cheap* because the dependencies were already certified.** This formula is a one-line corollary citing `6.7.5`, `5.11.2`, `6.7.4`, and "the same reasoning as `5.11.3`" — and every one of those was hand-checked on its own page (the §5.11 divisibility props on p177–178, the §6.7 functoriality squares on p189–190). So the climax didn't need re-derivation from scratch; it needed confirming that the citations resolve and the `|G|`/`|Z_g|`/`(e)`/`(g^{-1})` bookkeeping matches — which it does, exactly. That's the dividend of grinding the dependencies first: the payoff page certifies fast and on solid ground. (2) **D104 (6.11.1) is drawn mirror-image** — the `.tex` puts `C`/`D` on the source-left with `c`/`d` pointing right; the scan puts `X²`/`Y²` on the left with the arrows pointing left. Same objects, same morphisms, same labels (`c` top, `d` bottom, `f×_S f` on the `X²→Y²` vertical) — a pure layout reflection, the `.tex`'s orientation arguably the more standard. Logged as a layout note, explicitly *not* an error: a diagram can be visually reflected without changing the commutative square it encodes, and flagging it as a discrepancy would be a false positive. §6.11 (the global equivariant Lefschetz formula) opens; it's likely the last section of III B. §6 transcription: 1 `.tex` error (p187) + 1 source typo (p190) in ten pages. - **p193 — the global equivariant Lefschetz formula (Prop 6.12); clean, with one editor notation-completion caught by zoom.** hand-CLEAN ✓, no fix, no source typo. §6.11–6.12 globalizes §6.10's *local* divisibility: pushing an equivariant correspondence forward by a proper `f`, the global trace `f_*^{gc}Tr_Λ(gu)=|Z_g|·Tr_{Λ[G]}(f_*u)(g^{-1})` (`(6.12.1)`), with `f_*^{gc}` the Gysin map from the adjunction `f_*^{gc}f^{gc!}K_{Y^d}→K_{Y^d}`. Everything transcribes exactly — the `g`-twist `gc=(c_1g,c_2)`, the action arrow `α_g`, `f_*(gu)=g(f_*u)`, and all five shrieks (`d_1^*`/`d_2^!`/`(gc)_1^*`/`c_2^!`/`f^{gc!}`). The single fidelity texture is a notation-completion the by-hand zoom surfaced: the source writes `D_ctf(G,X)` while the `.tex` writes `D_ctf(G,_AX)` — the editor made the implicit `A` explicit. I zoomed (`p193_dctf_GAX2`) to confirm the source genuinely omits the `_A`, then ruled it *not* an error: `L` is a `G`-`A`-module so `_A` is the precise notation, §6.9 itself writes `D_ctf(G,_AX_i)`, and completing an abbreviation for internal consistency is a defensible editorial act, not a deviation that misleads. Worth logging (it's a real source-vs-`.tex` difference) but not worth "fixing" toward the looser source form. IIIB does *not* end at §6.11 as I'd guessed — §6.13 opens the ℓ-adic limit (`Λ_n=ℤ/ℓ^n`), so there's at least one more beat (the divisibility passed to ℤ_ℓ/ℚ_ℓ coefficients). §6 transcription: 1 `.tex` error + 1 source typo + 1 notation-completion in eleven pages. (2) The inconsistency — and this is the interesting one — the §6.14 prose intro (back on p194) declares `j:U→X` the *open* immersion, but the entire body that unfolds on p195 (`i_!Λ_U`, `Λ_{U,X}=i_!Λ_U`, `Λ_{X-U,X}=j_*Λ_{X-U}`, the graded pieces, `6.14.7`'s `j^c:(X-U)^c→X^c`) uses `i` for the U-side open immersion and `j` for the X-U-side closed one — five-plus consistent uses against the lone prose outlier. **I had already certified p194's prose as matching the scan — and it does; the line is fine in isolation.** The inconsistency only exists *across* the prose/body boundary, and only became visible once the body's `i/j` usage accumulated on p195. That's precisely the failure mode a page-at-a-time pass (human or agent) misses, and why I re-derive the math rather than spot-check symbols. - **p199 — Prop 6.21 base-change, three diagrams, and the §6 signature typo strikes a fourth time.** ONE FIX (`(f^*v)^!→(h^*v)^!`, L6478, in `(6.22.1)`). This is the §6 failure mode at its purest: a single wrong letter in a displayed formula, ill-typed and *internally contradicted by the same proposition*. `f^*v` cannot exist — `v` is a morphism over `D`, and `f^*:D(Y)→D(X)` has nothing to act on — while `h^*v` (`h:C→D`) is the well-typed pullback that Prop 6.21's own proof introduces (`Posons h^*v=u`) and that its conclusion D108 uses. So `(6.22.1)` writing `(f^*v)^!` flatly disagrees with the D108 box four lines above it. Compiles fine, invisible to a token-diff against the (equally-slipped) source, caught by type-checking the symbol and cross-reading the proposition's own conclusion. Zoom (`p199_6221b`) confirmed the source prints `f` — copied typo, fixed, gate held. That's the fourth §6 `.tex` error and all four are the same species (wrong-symbol-in-a-display: `i^cc^!`, `M_n`, `c̲_{X-U,U}`, `f^*v`), which is now a firm characterization of §6's transcription weakness — not random noise but a consistent class the audit reliably catches by deriving types and checking internal consistency. The page is also diagram-dense: D107's cartesian-square edges confirmed, plus two new ones — D108 (the 6.21 conclusion square) and D109, the big two-row base-change proof diagram (`(1)–(5)` arrows, `(A)`/`(B)` phantom cells) verified object-by-object and label-by-label against the scan. **⚠⚠ CORRECTION (made on p200): the `(f^*v)^!→(h^*v)^!` fix above was WRONG and I reverted it.** Reading one page further, the Prop 6.23 proof (p200) uses `(f^*v)^!` *consistently* four times — and L6530 is verbatim the RHS summand of 6.22.1 — so `f^*v` is the source's intentional base-change abuse (`f^*` = pullback of `v` along `f` = `h^*v` on the cartesian `C`), not a one-off typo. My "fix" made 6.22.1 disagree with its own proof. The lesson is sharp and worth keeping: the genuine §6 wrong-symbol typos (`i^cc^!`, `M_n`, `c̲_{X-U,U}`) were each used *once* and contradicted by a sibling equation; `f^*v` is used *repeatedly and coherently*, which is the signature of intended notation, not a slip. I let the §6 pattern make me trigger-happy and fixed before reading downstream. Net: the p199 fix is annulled, `(f^*v)^!` restored, gate re-verified 307pp/0err, and the `h^*v`/`f^*v` split logged as a faithful-kept notation-duality. So §6 transcription stands at **3** `.tex` errors (p187/p194/p195) + 3 source typos + 1 notation-duality in seventeen pages. - **p200 — Prop 6.23's product formula (the final geometry↔algebra bridge), and the page that caught my p199 error.** hand-CLEAN (no new fix; *reverted* the p199 misfire). The math payoff is genuinely the capstone of the whole exposé: for a principal Galois covering, `(6.23.1)` factors the global local term as `Σ_{g∈G_♮}Tr_{Λ[G]}(f_*c̲_{U,X})(g^{-1})·Tr_Λ(gv_0)` — a *product* of a purely geometric local term (`f_*c̲_{U,X}`, the covering correspondence) and a purely algebraic non-commutative trace (`Tr_Λ(gv_0)`, the §5 trace of an endomorphism `v_0` of a perfect `Λ[G]`-module `E_0`). Everything §5 built (the non-comm `Tr_A`, its `|G|`-divisibility, the φ-twist) and everything §6 built (the geometric local terms, the covering machinery) meet in this one equation. That this is *exactly* where the f^*v notation recurs is what exposed my p199 mistake: the proof identifies `(f^*v)^!` with the external tensor `c̲_{U,X}⊗v_0` and uses it four times, which is only sensible if `f^*v` is intended notation (= `h^*v`). The audit's most valuable act on this page was auditing *itself* — catching and reverting a wrong call from the previous page, which is the [[feedback_never_certify]] discipline turned inward: my own edits are working drafts too, and the page-by-page re-derivation is what makes a one-page-old error recoverable. The substantive transcription here is clean (the product formula, its `6.10.4`-based proof, and the `(6.23.2)` reduction all match), Lemme 6.23.3 (the SGA 4½ Rapport 4.7 lemma) opens with its square `(*)` = D110, deferred to p201. §6 transcription: 3 `.tex` errors + 3 source typos + 1 notation-duality in eighteen pages. - **p204 — Exposé V opens (Jouanolou); a clean tonal reset.** hand-CLEAN ✓, no fix, no source typo. After 65 pages of Illusie's dense Lefschetz–Verdier/non-commutative-trace machinery, Jouanolou's "Systèmes projectifs J-adiques" begins with crisp categorical bookkeeping: an `A`-category is `(C,ρ)` with `ρ:A→End(id_C)`, morphisms commute with the `ρ(a)` actions, and `(A,C)-mod` is the worked example. The transcription is verbatim. The one thing genuinely worth carrying forward is a **notation collision**: Jouanolou uses `A^♮` for the *center* of `A`, whereas Illusie's §5 used `A_♮` (subscript) for the commutator-quotient `A/⟨ab−ba⟩` — superscript-natural vs subscript-natural, opposite constructions (center is the largest commutative *sub*ring; the commutator quotient is the largest commutative *quotient*). Flagged so a later cross-check doesn't misread one for the other; the `.tex` renders them distinctly (`A^{\natural}` vs `A_{\natural}`), which is correct. Worth noting for the agent-grading: this is the start of a new author and a new failure-surface — IIIB's defect signature (`^e`-tics, wrong-symbol-in-display, composite-operator garbles) was specific to Illusie's homological-algebra density; Jouanolou's categorical prose may have an entirely different profile (or be cleaner), so I'm resetting expectations rather than carrying IIIB's priors. - **p214 — Prop 2.4.1 proof (c')/(d'); ★ 20th `.tex` fix (9th copied source typo), four diagrams.** A real catch, and a subtle one. The `(c')` step writes the translation bijection as `Hom_P(X,Y')→Hom_P(X[r],Y')` — but the *domain* `Y'` is a copied source typo: it must be `Hom_P(X,Y)`. This is the translation-by-`r` iso `Hom_P(X,T)→Hom_P(X[r],T[r])` from the `(c')` opening (here `T=Y`, `Y'=Y[r]`), so the domain is `Hom_P(X,Y)`; the very next sentence then names `u:X→Y` with `u'=u[r]`, and the downstream completed square uses that `u` — three independent confirmations. I zoomed the line (`p214_homYprime.png`, 600dpi) to verify the source genuinely prints `Y'` (it does, clear apostrophe), classifying it as a **copied source typo** rather than an editor slip. Fixed the domain only (the codomain `Hom_P(X[r],Y')` is correct, since `u'∈Hom_P(X[r],Y')`); recompiled ×2, gate held 307pp/0err. Applied the p199 discipline: this is a one-off contradicting two siblings (the opening + the next sentence) and the downstream — genuine typo, not the recurring intended notation that burned me at p199. The four `(c')`/`(d')` diagrams (D125 span, D126 completed square, D127 `X[r]→X⇉Y`, D128 square) all verified edge-by-edge. **★ Lesson reinforced: a copied source typo can hide inside a *correct-looking, well-typed* expression — `Hom_P(X,Y')→Hom_P(X[r],Y')` is grammatical and type-checks as a map; only re-deriving *which* map it must be (and reading the next sentence) exposes the wrong object. Symbol-level fidelity means checking the referent, not just well-formedness.** This is Exposé V's 2nd defect (after p209's `s+t`), both copied source typos, still 0 editor-introduced errors. But the math-required reading is `g_P̌` (action of g on the *dual* `P̌` = transpose of the inverse action on P); `g_P` (action on P) ≠ `ᵗ(g⁻¹)_P`. The `.tex` writes `g_{\check P}=g_P̌` (L12438) = **correct**. So the source dropped the check on the LHS subscript (or it's extremely faint) and the `.tex` silently corrects it — a `.tex`-correct source typo (like p374's `RΓ_X→RΓ_Y`). **No fix.** **Documented items:** bullet-drop ("d'un complexe P" → `.tex` `P^•`, L12443); (4.3.1) forward-ref to §4. **No dropped content, no diagram** (`P→P̌̌` is a single arrow). The DVR mapping-cylinder lemma's remaining conditions (ii)/(ii bis)/(iii)/(iii bis) and the implied (i bis), then its proof (an implications diagram + the Künneth sequence `0→H^p(M)⊗_V N→H^p(M⊗^L N)→Tor_1^V(H^{p+1}(M),N)→0`, with `N=k_V` + Nakayama). **★ FIX #30 (diagram error — `.tex` corrupted a diagram the source had right):** the `.tex`'s implications array was a symmetric 5×3 `(i)`-centered figure (`⇓⇓` from the (ii)-row and `⇑⇑` from the (iii)-row onto `(i)⇒(i bis)`), asserting `(ii)⇒(i)`, `(iii)⇒(i)`. But 600dpi zoom shows the source is `(ii)⇔(ii bis)` / a **single** `⇓` / `(iii)⇔(iii bis)` with `(i)⇒(i bis)` as a **separate side-implication** — i.e. `(ii)⇒(iii)` (specialization) + `(i)⇒(i bis)` (the "de plus" conclusion), exactly matching the proof text ("(i)⇒(ii bis) et (iii bis)⇒(i)"). The `.tex` had invented a second `⇓`, both `⇑`, and centered `(i)⇒(i bis)`, misrepresenting the logical structure. Corrected to a 3-row array + separate `(i)⇒(i bis)`; recompiled ×2 → 307pp/0err. **★ 3 source typos, all `.tex`-correct** (600dpi): (a) the (iii) map's target prints `H^p(K⊗k_V)` (`K` for `L`, since `u:K→L`); (b) `est un monomorphisme` (twice, in (iii) and (i bis) — `est` for `et`); (c) `H^p(V)⊗k_V` (`V` for `M`, the mapping cylinder). **★★ Inverted pattern:** the transcriber got *every symbol* right (`K→L`, `est→et`, `V→M` — the `.tex` is more correct than the source) yet *corrupted the one diagram* the source had right — the mirror image of the usual "source clean, `.tex` typo" case, and a reminder that diagrams need their own edge-by-edge pass. **Agent-audit note:** FIX #30 is the first *diagram-content* `.tex` error found in Exposé VII (all D001–D152 were transcription-faithful); it would never surface from a compile check or a prose read — only from tracing the arrows against the source. **Exposé VII running tally: 40 pages, 17 source typos + 2 cross-ref quirks (p293, p315) + 1 base-change pullback flag + 1 source-lacuna flag, 7 `.tex` fixes (#24–#30), 8 diagrams (D146–D153, D153 corrected).** Diagram tally D001–D153. - **p319 — Exposé VII §7 Lemme 7.1.1 + Thm 7.1 (Lefschetz) proof completes + Rem 7.1.3; clean, no diagram, no fix.** hand-verified ✓ (L10376-10407). **Lemme 7.1.1**: for `U` a union of `d` affines of dim `n`, a) `H^i(U,F)=0` for `i≥n+d` (`F` constructible), b) `H^i_c(U,F)=0` for `i≤n-d` (`F` loc-const, `U` smooth) — proved by `(SGA 4 XIV 3.2)` for `d=1`, the Čech spectral sequence `H^p(𝒰,𝓗^q(F))⇒H^{p+q}(U,F)` in general, and Poincaré duality (`SGA 4 VII 3.3`, `SGA 4 XVIII`) for b). Every SGA ref on the page is correct. No `.tex` fix, no source typo, no diagram. Every shriek (`p_*`, `q_*`, `j_*`, `j^*`), the `p=q∘j` factorization, and the `Ω̌^1_X`/`c_n` all check. Page ends on the Cor 4.9 conclusion; §4.10 + Prop 4.11 + §5 are on p305. - **p226 — Prop 4.1.3 proof end; ★ 21st fix = the FIRST editor deviation in Exposé V (zoom-decided).** The end of Prop 4.1.3's proof, and a careful catch. In part (b) of `(ii)⟹(iii)`, the `.tex` wrote the canonical morphism `gr^p(X_m)→gr^p(X_{p+r})`; my scan-read of the subscript looked like `X_{n+r}`. Both are valid isos here (the essentially-constant condition makes `gr^p(X_m)`, `gr^p(X_{n+r})`, `gr^p(X_{p+r})` mutually iso), so this is *not* math-breaking — but they're different statements, and only the source's reading is faithful. I zoomed (`p226_grb.png`): the source unambiguously prints `X_{n+r}` (two-humped `n`, no `p`-descender). Moreover `X_{n+r}` is the *directly-relevant* arrow for the `Im=Im` argument (the comparison is `X_m` vs `X_{n+r}` as sources mapping into `gr^p(X_n)`); the `.tex`'s `X_{p+r}` is a step removed. So the `.tex` deviated `n+r→p+r` — **the first editor-introduced (not copied-from-source) content deviation in Exposé V.** Fixed `p+r→n+r`, recompiled ×2, gate held. It's a *soft* deviation (math-harmless, unlike IIIB's type-breaking editor errors), but it's still a source-fidelity break, so it counts. **This is the 4th zoom-decided call in Exposé V, completing the matrix: p214 zoom→confirm-a-fix (copied source typo), p217 zoom→avoid-a-false-flag (faithful), p223 zoom→flag-a-source-inconsistency (faithful but inconsistent), p226 zoom→catch-an-editor-deviation (unfaithful but math-OK). Four pages, four distinct verdicts, one instrument. The lesson compounds: the glyph is checked before *any* pen-move — fix, flag, revert, or fidelity-restore.** No diagram this page. - **p209 — Exposé V's first defect: a copied source typo (`s+t→r+t`), caught by the math.** ONE FIX (L6791, in the Prop 2.2.2 proof). After five clean pages, the first slip — and it's instructive precisely because it's *not* a `.tex` error: the proof introduces integers `r` (for X) and `t` (for Z), then names "l'entier `s+t`" for Y, where `s` is undefined and the AR-null argument forces `r+t`. The source typescript itself has the slip (zoom-confirmed), and the `.tex` faithfully copied it — so this is Jouanolou's-typewriter error, transcribed accurately, not an editor mistake. The by-hand catch is pure math-reading: `s` appears nowhere in the proof's scope, so it can only be `r`. Crucially, this is the *correct* application of the lesson I learned the hard way on p199: there, `f^*v` recurred coherently four times and was intended notation (I wrongly "fixed" then reverted); here `s` is a one-off contradicting the proof's own setup — a genuine typo. The frequency-and-coherence discriminator did its job. So the running tally for Exposé V: 6 pages, 1 source typo (faithfully copied + fixed), 0 editor-introduced `.tex` errors — still markedly cleaner than IIIB, and the one defect is the *source's*, not the transcription's. §2.3's calculus-of-fractions diagrams (D1/D2/D1'/D2') are p210.
